=== Changed :: CHANGELOG.md ===
L7: BASE | Sodium [SodiumVersion]() for Minecraft [MCVersion]() makes some improvements to the options UI, introduces a new automatic publishing system, fixes some bugs, and introduces some small performance optimizations.
L7: REPO | Sodium [SodiumVersion]() fixes a number of bugs, adds some new features, and improves memory usage. The release includes a new display of FPS percentiles which more accurately represent what you actually want to know about the FPS.
L8: BASE | 
L8: REPO | - The median (labeled p50) shows the typical frame rate ignoring the slow frames
L9: BASE | - Workflow and Gradle based publishing setup with changelog system ([#3575](https://github.com/CaffeineMC/sodium/pull/3575), [#3622](https://github.com/CaffeineMC/sodium/pull/3622))
L9: REPO | - The 98th and 99.5th percentiles show the FPS of slow and very slow frames
L10: BASE | - Invalidate cached search index sources to be properly indexed after language switch
L10: REPO | The average, which is what Minecraft displays by default, can get pulled down by few slow frames even though otherwise the frame rate is pretty high. This leads to the average FPS being not very representative of the actual frame rate you're seeing most of the time.
L11: BASE | - Jump to the mod's options when its header in the page list is clicked
L11: REPO | 
L12: BASE | - Show that an entry in the options page list leads to an external page more clearly
L12: REPO | The new option to sort entities quads by their closest point allows correct rendering of similar translucent entities that have specific types of otherwise wrongly sorted geometry. This is disabled by default as it incurs a performance cost.
L13: BASE | - Hide options search bar clear button when search bar is empty

L165: REPO | Each mod adds a page for its options, within each page there are groups of options, and each group contains a list of options. Each option has an id, a name, a tooltip, a storage handler, a binding, and a default value. There are three types of options: boolean (tickbox), integer (slider), and enum. L167: REPO | Some attributes of an option can be provided dynamically, meaning the returned value can depend on the state of another option. The default value, option enablement, and the allowed values can be computed dynamically. The methods for setting a dynamic value provider also require you to specify a list of dependencies, which are the resource locations of the options that the dynamic value provider reads from. Since dynamically evaluated attributes may change the state of an option, cyclic dependencies will lead to option registration failing and the game crashing.

L181: REPO | ### Using `ConfigBuilder` and `ModOptions`
L182: BASE | #### NeoForge: With an Annotation
L182: REPO | 
L183: BASE | 
L183: REPO | The `ConfigBuilder` instance passed to the registration method allows quick and easy registration of a mod's own options using `ConfigBuilder.registerOwnModOptions`. The mod's id, name, version or a formatter for the existing version, and the color theme can be configured on the returned `ModOptionsBuilder`. It's also possible to register options for additional mods using `ConfigBuilder.registerModOptions`. Which mod is the "own" mod for `registerOwnModOptions` is determined by the mod that owns the metadata-based entrypoint.
L184: BASE | Since NeoForge has the convention of using annotations for entrypoints, this option is provided as an alternative. Any classes annotated with `@ConfigEntryPointForge("examplemod")` will be loaded as config entrypoints too. Note that the annotation must be given the mod id that should be associated as the default mod for which a config is registered with `ConfigBuilder.registerOwnModOptions`. This is necessary as it's otherwise impossible to uniquely determine which mod a class is associated with on NeoForge.
L184: REPO | 
L185: BASE | 
L185: REPO | Each registered mod gets its own header in the page list. The color of the header and the corresponding entries is randomly selected from a predefined list by default, but can be customized using `ModOptionsBuilder.setColorTheme`. A color theme is created either by specifying three (A)RGB colors or a single base color with the lighter and darker colors getting derived automatically. A mod can also specify an icon with `ModOptionsBuilder.setIcon`, which takes a `Identifier` pointing to a texture, which will be tinted in the theme color and rendered in its entirety as a square. Icons set with `ModOptionsBuilder.setNonTintedIcon` are not tinted.
L186: BASE | ```java
L186: REPO | 
L187: BASE | import net.caffeinemc.mods.sodium.api.config.ConfigEntryPoint;
L187: REPO | To simply switch to a new `Screen` when an entry in the video settings screen's page list is clicked, use `ConfigBuilder.createExternalPage` and add the returned page normally after configuring it with a name and a `Consumer<Screen>` that receives the current screen and switches to your custom screen.
L188: BASE | import net.caffeinemc.mods.sodium.api.config.ConfigEntryPointForge;

L196: BASE | ### Registering Your Options
L196: REPO | 
L197: BASE | 
L197: REPO | Flags set with `OptionBuilder.setFlags` control what things are reset when this option is applied. They include reloading chunks or reloading resource packs. See `OptionFlag` for the available values.
L198: BASE | Each mod adds a page for its options, within each page there are groups of options, and each group contains a list of options. Each option has an id, a name, a tooltip, a storage handler, a binding, and a default value. There are three types of options: boolean (tickbox), integer (slider), and enum. Optionally, all types of options can be disabled, while integer and enum options can have their allowed values restricted. Those two types also require you to set a function that assigns a label to each selected value.
L198: REPO | 
L199: BASE | 
L199: REPO | The default value set with `OptionBuilder.setDefaultValue`, or dynamically with `OptionBuilder.setDefaultProvider`, is used if the value returned by the binding does not fulfill the option's value constraint (in the case of an integer or enum option).
L200: BASE | Some attributes of an option can be provided dynamically, meaning the returned value can depend on the state of another option. The default value, option enablement, and the allowed values can be computed dynamically. The methods for setting a dynamic value provider also require you to specify a list of dependencies, which are the resource locations of the options that the dynamic value provider reads from. Since dynamically evaluated attributes may change the state of an option, cyclic dependencies will lead to option registration failing and the game crashing.
L200: REPO | 
L201: BASE | 
L201: REPO | Disabling an option with `OptionBuilder.setEnabled(false)` shows the option as strikethrough and makes it non-interactive. Otherwise, especially with regard to value constraints, it will behave as usual.
L202: BASE | Sodium constructs one instance of the entrypoint class, and then calls the early and late registration methods at the right time.
L202: REPO | 
L203: BASE | 
L203: REPO | The binding configured with `OptionBuilder.setBinding` is called when changes to the options have been made and are applied, or when the value no longer fulfills the option's constraints and is reset to the default value. It's also used to initially load a value during initialization.
L204: BASE | ### Icon Design
L204: REPO | 
L205: BASE | 
L205: REPO | The hook given with `OptionBuilder.setApplyHook` is run after the option has been saved if its valued changed. The hook is given access to the current `ConfigState`, without needing to declare dependencies since the hook does not return anything. This is equivalent to making a singleton custom flag for this option and registering a flag hook for it, and it is implemented this way internally.
L206: BASE | The UI is designed with monochrome, binary-alpha icons in mind. These icons are tinted the color of each mod's theme. We recommend that API users avoid displaying an icon that isn't appropriately styled. However, users can choose to deviate from this convention by presenting a regular, full-color icon using the means provided in the API.
L206: REPO | 
L207: BASE | 
L207: REPO | ### Using `? extends OptionBuilder`
L208: BASE | It is not necessary to explicitly set a theme color, as the system will automatically select one from a predefined set. However, if you set a custom theme color, it should have a minimum level of saturation to remain true to its purpose as an accent color. For aesthetic and accessibility reasons, it is also not reasonable to set the brightness/value of the theme color to be too dark. The theme base color should also not be too bright, otherwise the highlight color, which is brighter, will have no contrast against it.
L208: REPO | 
L209: BASE | 
L209: REPO | Some of the attributes of an option are required and you must set them, or registration will fail. The concrete extensions of `OptionBuilder` for each of the option types have some additional methods for configuring type-specific things, some of which are also required. Notably, `EnumOptionBuilder.setElementNameProvider` and `IntegerOptionBuilder.setValueFormatter` are required in order to display these types of options. The method `setValueFormatter` for integer options takes a `ControlValueFormatter`, which simply formats a number as a `Component`. Many standard value formatters are provided in `ControlValueFormatterImpls` (not part of the API package).
L210: BASE | ## API Notes
L210: REPO | 
L211: BASE | 
L211: REPO | Integer and enum options can have value constraints that restrict the set of allowed values the user can select. For integer options, a `Range` must be configured with `IntegerOptionBuilder.setRange` so that the slider's start, end, and step positions are well-defined. Enum options may be configured to only allow the selection of certain elements with `EnumOptionBuilder.setAllowedValues`.
L212: BASE | The API is largely self-explanatory and an example is provided above. Also see Sodium's own options registration for a more in-depth example of the API's usage.
L212: REPO | 
L213: BASE | 
L213: REPO | ### Using Option Overlays and Replacement
L214: BASE | ### Using `ConfigBuilder` and `ModOptions`
L214: REPO | 
L215: BASE | 
L215: REPO | It's a typical use case to want to change one of Sodium's or another mod's options to have a different range or behavior. This can be achieved in one of two ways: by replacing the option or by applying an overlay to it.
L216: BASE | The `ConfigBuilder` instance passed to the registration method allows quick and easy registration of a mod's own options using `ConfigBuilder.registerOwnModOptions`. The mod's id, name, version or a formatter for the existing version, and the color theme can be configured on the returned `ModOptionsBuilder`. It's also possible to register options for additional mods using `ConfigBuilder.registerModOptions`. Which mod is the "own" mod for `registerOwnModOptions` is determined by the mod that owns the metadata-based entrypoint or the mod id passed to the `@ConfigEntryPointForge("examplemod")` annotation.
L216: REPO | 
L217: BASE | 
L217: REPO | #### Replacement
L218: BASE | Each registered mod gets its own header in the page list. The color of the header and the corresponding entries is randomly selected from a predefined list by default, but can be customized using `ModOptionsBuilder.setColorTheme`. A color theme is created either by specifying three (A)RGB colors or a single base color with the lighter and darker colors getting derived automatically. A mod can also specify an icon with `ModOptionsBuilder.setIcon`, which takes a `Identifier` pointing to a texture, which will be tinted in the theme color and rendered in its entirety as a square. Icons set with `ModOptionsBuilder.setNonTintedIcon` are not tinted.
L218: REPO | 
L219: BASE | 
L219: REPO | You can replace another option with `ModOptionsBuilder.registerOptionReplacement` by supplying a target identifier and a new option built as usual. The ID of the new option replaces the ID of the old option, and you can optionally choose to adopt the old option's ID to remain a target for overlays targeting it.
L220: BASE | To simply switch to a new `Screen` when an entry in the video settings screen's page list is clicked, use `ConfigBuilder.createExternalPage` and add the returned page normally after configuring it with a name and a `Consumer<Screen>` that receives the current screen and switches to your custom screen.
L220: REPO | 
L221: BASE | 
L221: REPO | #### Overlays
L222: BASE | ### Using `OptionBuilder`
L222: REPO | 
L223: BASE | 
L223: REPO | Changing only some aspects of an option is possible through `ModOptionsBuilder.registerOptionOverlay`. You supply a partial option by using the same option builder as one usually does. The properties of the target options are replaced with the new ones if specified.
L224: BASE | The storage handler set with `OptionBuilder.setStorageHandler` is called after changes have been made to the options through the bindings. This lets you flush the changes to the config file once, instead of every time an option is changed.
L224: REPO | 
L225: BASE | 
L225: REPO | ### Custom Flags
L226: BASE | The tooltip set with `OptionBuilder.setTooltip` can optionally be a function that generates a tooltip depending on the option's current value. This is useful for enum options for which the description would be too long otherwise.
L226: REPO | 
L227: BASE | 
L227: REPO | By registering a flag hook with `ModOptionsBuilder.registerFlagHook` you can run code after a flag from the specified set is triggered. After an option is applied and its value has been saved, all of its attached flags are triggered. The st of trigger flags for `registerFlagHook` can include custom flags, which are simply an arbitrary `Identifier`, or one of the meta flags, such as `OptionFlag.REQUIRES_RENDERER_RELOAD.getId()`.
L228: BASE | Optionally a performance impact can be specified with `OptionBuilder.setImpact` where the impact ranges from low to high (or "varies").
L228: REPO | 
L229: BASE | 
L229: REPO | ### Dependent Values
L230: BASE | Flags set with `OptionBuilder.setFlags` control what things are reset when this option is applied. They include reloading chunks or reloading resource packs. See `OptionFlag` for the available values.
L230: REPO | 
L231: BASE | 
L231: REPO | To use a dynamic value method of the form `set___Provider`, you need to pass it a value provider. This provider is called with a reference to the current state of the options store. In order to be allowed to read the values of other options from it, you need to pass the IDs of the options this provider depends on as arguments to the method. Reading from options that are not declared as a dependency throws an exception. The system makes sure no dynamic value of an option depends on its own value or other options' values that indirectly depend on the first option, i.e. cycles are forbidden.
L232: BASE | The default value set with `OptionBuilder.setDefaultValue`, or dynamically with `OptionBuilder.setDefaultProvider`, is used if the value returned by the binding does not fulfill the option's value constraint (in the case of an integer or enum option).
L232: REPO | 
L233: BASE | 
L233: REPO | #### Special Dependencies
L234: BASE | Disabling an option with `OptionBuilder.setEnabled(false)` shows the option as strikethrough and makes it non-interactive. Otherwise, especially with regard to value constraints, it will behave as usual.
L234: REPO | 
L235: BASE | 
L235: REPO | Providers that declare a dependency on `ConfigState.UPDATE_ON_REBUILD` are refreshed when the UI is rebuilt.
L236: BASE | The binding configured with `OptionBuilder.setBinding` is called when changes to the options have been made and are applied, or when the value no longer fulfills the option's constraints and is reset to the default value. It's also used to initially load a value during initialization.
L236: REPO | 
L237: BASE | 
L237: REPO | Providers that declare a dependency on `ConfigState.UPDATE_ON_APPLY` are refreshed when the modified value of the option they are attached to is applied. This is the only situation in which a dynamic valued property of an option is allowed to depend on its containing options' value.
L238: BASE | The hook given with `OptionBuilder.setApplyHook` is run after the option has been saved if its valued changed. The hook is given access to the current `ConfigState`, without needing to declare dependencies since the hook does not return anything. This is equivalent to making a singleton custom flag for this option and registering a flag hook for it, and it is implemented this way internally.
L238: REPO | 
L239: BASE | 
L239: REPO | Both of these special dependencies are typically not needed and making use of them is most likely user error. Sodium only uses them for making sure the extremely weird GUI Scale option behaves as expected.
L240: BASE | ### Using `? extends OptionBuilder`
L240: REPO | <NO LINE>
L241: BASE | 
L241: REPO | <NO LINE>
L242: BASE | Some of the attributes of an option are required and you must set them, or registration will fail. The concrete extensions of `OptionBuilder` for each of the option types have some additional methods for configuring type-specific things, some of which are also required. Notably, `EnumOptionBuilder.setElementNameProvider` and `IntegerOptionBuilder.setValueFormatter` are required in order to display these types of options. The method `setValueFormatter` for integer options takes a `ControlValueFormatter`, which simply formats a number as a `Component`. Many standard value formatters are provided in `ControlValueFormatterImpls` (not part of the API package).
L242: REPO | <NO LINE>
L243: BASE | 
L243: REPO | <NO LINE>
L244: BASE | Integer and enum options can have value constraints that restrict the set of allowed values the user can select. For integer options, a `Range` must be configured with `IntegerOptionBuilder.setRange` so that the slider's start, end, and step positions are well-defined. Enum options may be configured to only allow the selection of certain elements with `EnumOptionBuilder.setAllowedValues`.

L246: BASE | ### Using Option Overlays and Replacement
L246: REPO | <NO LINE>
L247: BASE | 
L247: REPO | <NO LINE>
L248: BASE | It's a typical use case to want to change one of Sodium's or another mod's options to have a different range or behavior. This can be achieved in one of two ways: by replacing the option or by applying an overlay to it.
L248: REPO | <NO LINE>
L249: BASE | 
L249: REPO | <NO LINE>
L250: BASE | #### Replacement
L250: REPO | <NO LINE>
L251: BASE | 
L251: REPO | <NO LINE>
L252: BASE | You can replace another option with `ModOptionsBuilder.registerOptionReplacement` by supplying a target identifier and a new option built as usual. The ID of the new option replaces the ID of the old option, and you can optionally choose to adopt the old option's ID to remain a target for overlays targeting it.
L252: REPO | <NO LINE>
L253: BASE | 
L253: REPO | <NO LINE>
L254: BASE | #### Overlays
L254: REPO | <NO LINE>
L255: BASE | 
L255: REPO | <NO LINE>
L256: BASE | Changing only some aspects of an option is possible through `ModOptionsBuilder.registerOptionOverlay`. You supply a partial option by using the same option builder as one usually does. The properties of the target options are replaced with the new ones if specified.
L256: REPO | <NO LINE>
L257: BASE | 
L257: REPO | <NO LINE>
L258: BASE | ### Custom Flags
L258: REPO | <NO LINE>
L259: BASE | 
L259: REPO | <NO LINE>
L260: BASE | By registering a flag hook with `ModOptionsBuilder.registerFlagHook` you can run code after a flag from the specified set is triggered. After an option is applied and its value has been saved, all of its attached flags are triggered. The st of trigger flags for `registerFlagHook` can include custom flags, which are simply an arbitrary `Identifier`, or one of the meta flags, such as `OptionFlag.REQUIRES_RENDERER_RELOAD.getId()`.
L260: REPO | <NO LINE>
L261: BASE | 
L261: REPO | <NO LINE>
L262: BASE | ### Dependent Values
L262: REPO | <NO LINE>
L263: BASE | 
L263: REPO | <NO LINE>
L264: BASE | To use a dynamic value method of the form `set___Provider`, you need to pass it a value provider. This provider is called with a reference to the current state of the options store. In order to be allowed to read the values of other options from it, you need to pass the IDs of the options this provider depends on as arguments to the method. Reading from options that are not declared as a dependency throws an exception. The system makes sure no dynamic value of an option depends on its own value or other options' values that indirectly depend on the first option, i.e. cycles are forbidden.
L264: REPO | <NO LINE>
L265: BASE | 
L265: REPO | <NO LINE>
L266: BASE | #### Special Dependencies
L266: REPO | <NO LINE>
L267: BASE | 
L267: REPO | <NO LINE>
L268: BASE | Providers that declare a dependency on `ConfigState.UPDATE_ON_REBUILD` are refreshed when the UI is rebuilt.
L268: REPO | <NO LINE>
L269: BASE | 
L269: REPO | <NO LINE>
L270: BASE | Providers that declare a dependency on `ConfigState.UPDATE_ON_APPLY` are refreshed when the modified value of the option they are attached to is applied. This is the only situation in which a dynamic valued property of an option is allowed to depend on its containing options' value.
L270: REPO | <NO LINE>
L271: BASE | 
L271: REPO | <NO LINE>
L272: BASE | Both of these special dependencies are typically not needed and making use of them is most likely user error. Sodium only uses them for making sure the extremely weird GUI Scale option behaves as expected.
L272: REPO | <NO LINE>

L285: REPO |             this.outOfWorldRadius = 0;
L286: BASE |             // visit queued sections here instead of before they are queued to get correct path information as visibility of different types may be added to a section at different times.
L286: REPO |             this.outOfWorldHeight = this.level.getMaxSectionY();

L287: REPO |             this.outOfWorldDirection = GraphDirection.UP;
L288: BASE |             boolean wasInFrustum = false;
L288: REPO |         } else {
L289: BASE |             if (incomingDirectionsRegular != 0) {
L289: REPO |             this.outOfWorldRadius = -1;
L290: BASE |                 if (incomingDirectionsLocal != 0) {
L290: REPO |             this.initWithinWorld(visitor, queue, viewport, useOcclusionCulling, frame);
L291: BASE |                     wasInFrustum = this.visitorLocal.visitTestVisible(section);
L291: REPO |         }
L292: BASE |                 }
L292: REPO |     }
L293: BASE |                 this.visitorRegular.visit(section, wasInFrustum);
L293: REPO | 
L294: BASE |             }
L294: REPO |     private void initWithinWorld(RenderSectionVisitor visitor, WriteQueue<RenderSection> queue, Viewport viewport, boolean useOcclusionCulling, int frame) {
L295: BASE |             this.visitorWide.visit(section, wasInFrustum);
L295: REPO |         var origin = viewport.getChunkCoord();
L296: BASE | 
L296: REPO |         var section = this.getRenderSection(origin.getX(), origin.getY(), origin.getZ());
L297: BASE |             int outgoingWide, outgoingRegular, outgoingLocal;
L297: REPO | 
L298: BASE | 
L298: REPO |         if (section == null) {
L299: BASE |             if (this.useOcclusionCulling) {
L299: REPO |             return;
L300: BASE |                 var visibilityDataSet = section.getVisibilityData();
L300: REPO |         }
L301: BASE |                 if (visibilityDataSet == null) {
L301: REPO | 
L302: BASE |                     // No visibility data, so we can't traverse into any neighbors.
L302: REPO |         section.setLastVisibleFrame(frame);
L303: BASE |                     continue;
L303: REPO |         section.setIncomingDirections(GraphDirectionSet.NONE);
L304: BASE |                 }
L304: REPO | 
L305: BASE | 
L305: REPO |         visitor.visit(section);
L306: BASE |                 // get the visibility data for the camera perspective relative to this section
L306: REPO | 
L307: BASE |                 var visibilityDataWide = this.joinVisibilityData(visibilityDataSet, section, this.viewport, 2);
L307: REPO |         int outgoing;
L308: BASE |                 var visibilityDataRegular = this.joinVisibilityData(visibilityDataSet, section, this.viewport, 1);
L308: REPO | 
L309: BASE |                 var visibilityDataLocal = this.joinVisibilityData(visibilityDataSet, section, this.viewport, 0);
L309: REPO |         if (useOcclusionCulling) {
L310: BASE | 
L310: REPO |             // Since the camera is located inside this chunk, there are no "incoming" directions. So we need to instead
L311: BASE |                 // occlude paths through the section if it's being viewed at an angle where
L311: REPO |             // find any possible paths out of this chunk and enqueue those neighbors.
L312: BASE |                 // the other side can't possibly be seen
L312: REPO |             outgoing = VisibilityEncoding.getConnections(section.getVisibilityData());
L313: BASE |                 visibilityDataWide &= getAngleVisibilityMaskWide(this.viewport, section, 2);
L313: REPO |         } else {
L314: BASE |                 visibilityDataRegular &= getAngleVisibilityMaskWide(this.viewport, section, 1);
L314: REPO |             // Occlusion culling is disabled, so we can traverse into any neighbor.
L315: BASE |                 visibilityDataLocal &= getAngleVisibilityMaskLocal(this.viewport, section);
L315: REPO |             outgoing = GraphDirectionSet.ALL;
L316: BASE | 
L316: REPO |         }
L317: BASE |                 // When using occlusion culling, we can only traverse into neighbors for which there is a path of
L317: REPO | 
L318: BASE |                 // visibility through this chunk. This is determined by taking all the incoming paths to this chunk and
L318: REPO |         visitNeighbors(queue, section, outgoing, frame);
L319: BASE |                 // creating a union of the outgoing paths from those.
L319: REPO |     }
L321: BASE |                 outgoingWide = VisibilityEncoding.getConnections(visibilityDataWide, incomingDirectionsWide);
L321: REPO |     // Enqueues sections that are inside the viewport using diamond spiral iteration to avoid sorting and ensure a
L322: BASE |                 outgoingRegular = VisibilityEncoding.getConnections(visibilityDataRegular, incomingDirectionsRegular);
L322: REPO |     // consistent order. Innermost layers are enqueued first. Within each layer, iteration starts at the northernmost
L323: BASE |                 outgoingLocal = VisibilityEncoding.getConnections(visibilityDataLocal, incomingDirectionsLocal);
L323: REPO |     // section and proceeds counterclockwise (N->W->S->E).
L324: BASE |             } else {
L324: REPO |     private boolean initOutsideWorldHeight(WriteQueue<RenderSection> queue,
L325: BASE |                 // Not using any occlusion culling, so traversing in any direction is legal.
L325: REPO |                                         Viewport viewport,
L326: BASE |                 outgoingWide = GraphDirectionSet.ALL;
L326: REPO |                                         float searchDistance,
L327: BASE |                 outgoingRegular = GraphDirectionSet.ALL;
L327: REPO |                                         int frame) {
L328: BASE |                 outgoingLocal = GraphDirectionSet.ALL;
L328: REPO |         var origin = viewport.getChunkCoord();
L329: BASE |             }
L329: REPO |         var radius = Mth.floor(searchDistance / 16.0f);
L330: BASE | 
L330: REPO |         var height = this.outOfWorldHeight;
L331: BASE |             // We can only traverse *outwards* from the center of the graph search, so mask off any invalid directions.
L331: REPO |         var direction = this.outOfWorldDirection;
L332: BASE |             outgoingWide &= getOutwardDirectionsWide(origin, section);
L332: REPO |         var layer = this.outOfWorldRadius;
L333: BASE |             outgoingRegular &= getOutwardDirectionsRegular(origin, section);
L333: REPO | 
L334: BASE |             outgoingLocal &= getOutwardDirectionsRegular(origin, section);
L334: REPO |         // Layer 0
L335: BASE | 
L335: REPO |         if (layer == 0) {
L336: BASE |             visitNeighbors(writeQueue, section, outgoingWide, outgoingRegular, outgoingLocal, this.inBoundsOrigin);
L336: REPO |             this.tryVisitNode(queue, origin.getX(), height, origin.getZ(), direction, frame, viewport);
L338: BASE |     }
L338: REPO | 
L339: BASE | 
L339: REPO |         // Complete layers, excluding layer 0
L340: BASE |     private long joinVisibilityData(long[] visibilityDataSet, RenderSection section, Viewport viewport, int width) {
L340: REPO |         else if (layer <= radius) {
L341: BASE |         if (visibilityDataSet.length == 1) {
L341: REPO |             for (int z = -layer; z < layer; z++) {
L342: BASE |             return visibilityDataSet[0];
L342: REPO |                 int x = Math.abs(z) - layer;
L343: BASE |         }
L343: REPO |                 this.tryVisitNode(queue, origin.getX() + x, height, origin.getZ() + z, direction, frame, viewport);
L344: BASE | 
L344: REPO |             }
L345: BASE |         int directionSets;
L345: REPO | 
L346: BASE |         if (width == 0) {
L346: REPO |             for (int z = layer; z > -layer; z--) {
L347: BASE |             directionSets = getDirectionSetsLocal(viewport, section);
L347: REPO |                 int x = layer - Math.abs(z);
L348: BASE |         } else {
L348: REPO |                 this.tryVisitNode(queue, origin.getX() + x, height, origin.getZ() + z, direction, frame, viewport);
L349: BASE |             directionSets = getDirectionSetsWide(viewport, section, width);
L349: REPO |             }
L352: BASE |         // Combine the relevant visibility data sets.
L352: REPO |         // Incomplete layers
L353: BASE |         // Since each perspective can be seen from two opposite sides, two bits in each mask are set.
L353: REPO |         else if (layer <= 2 * radius) {
L354: BASE |         long visibilityData = 0L;
L354: REPO |             int l = layer - radius;
L355: BASE |         if ((directionSets & 0b10000001) != 0) {
L355: REPO | 
L356: BASE |             visibilityData |= visibilityDataSet[0];
L356: REPO |             for (int z = -radius; z <= -l; z++) {
L357: BASE |         }
L357: REPO |                 int x = -z - layer;
L358: BASE |         if ((directionSets & 0b01000010) != 0) {
L358: REPO |                 this.tryVisitNode(queue, origin.getX() + x, height, origin.getZ() + z, direction, frame, viewport);
L359: BASE |             visibilityData |= visibilityDataSet[1];
L359: REPO |             }
L360: BASE |         }
L360: REPO | 
L361: BASE |         if ((directionSets & 0b00100100) != 0) {
L361: REPO |             for (int z = l; z <= radius; z++) {
L362: BASE |             visibilityData |= visibilityDataSet[2];
L362: REPO |                 int x = z - layer;
L363: BASE |         }
L363: REPO |                 this.tryVisitNode(queue, origin.getX() + x, height, origin.getZ() + z, direction, frame, viewport);
L364: BASE |         if ((directionSets & 0b00011000) != 0) {
L364: REPO |             }
L365: BASE |             visibilityData |= visibilityDataSet[3];
L365: REPO | 
L366: BASE |         }
L366: REPO |             for (int z = radius; z >= l; z--) {
L367: BASE | 
L367: REPO |                 int x = layer - z;
L368: BASE |         return visibilityData;
L368: REPO |                 this.tryVisitNode(queue, origin.getX() + x, height, origin.getZ() + z, direction, frame, viewport);
L369: BASE |     }
L369: REPO |             }
L371: BASE |     private void visitNeighbors(WriteQueue<RenderSection> queue, RenderSection section, int outgoingWide, int outgoingRegular, int outgoingLocal, SectionPos origin) {
L371: REPO |             for (int z = -l; z >= -radius; z--) {
L372: BASE |         var adjacentMask = section.getAdjacentMask();
L372: REPO |                 int x = layer + z;
L373: BASE |         outgoingWide &= adjacentMask;
L373: REPO |                 this.tryVisitNode(queue, origin.getX() + x, height, origin.getZ() + z, direction, frame, viewport);
L374: BASE | 
L374: REPO |             }
L375: BASE |         // Check if there are any valid connections left, and if not, early-exit.
L375: REPO |         }
L376: BASE |         // if wide doesn't have any connections, the others won't either
L376: REPO | 
L377: BASE |         if (outgoingWide == GraphDirectionSet.NONE) {
L377: REPO |         // nothing more to init
L378: BASE |             return;
L378: REPO |         else {
L379: BASE |         }
L379: REPO |             return false;
L380: BASE |         outgoingRegular &= adjacentMask;
L380: REPO |         }
L381: BASE |         outgoingLocal &= adjacentMask;
L381: REPO |         return true;
L382: BASE | 
L382: REPO |     }
L383: BASE |         var originSection = origin == null ? null : section;
L383: REPO | 
L384: BASE | 
L384: REPO |     private void tryVisitNode(WriteQueue<RenderSection> queue, int x, int y, int z, int direction, int frame, Viewport viewport) {
L385: BASE |         // the viewpoint is outside the world, so the angle computations relying on propagating angle information
L385: REPO |         RenderSection section = this.getRenderSection(x, y, z);
L386: BASE |         // from the origin section to the others won't work.
L386: REPO | 
L387: BASE |         tryVisitNode(queue, originSection, outgoingWide, outgoingRegular, outgoingLocal, section.adjacentDown, GraphDirection.DOWN);
L387: REPO |         if (section == null || !isWithinFrustum(viewport, section)) {
L388: BASE |         tryVisitNode(queue, originSection, outgoingWide, outgoingRegular, outgoingLocal, section.adjacentUp, GraphDirection.UP);
L388: REPO |             return;
L389: BASE |         tryVisitNode(queue, originSection, outgoingWide, outgoingRegular, outgoingLocal, section.adjacentNorth, GraphDirection.NORTH);
L389: REPO |         }
L390: BASE |         tryVisitNode(queue, originSection, outgoingWide, outgoingRegular, outgoingLocal, section.adjacentSouth, GraphDirection.SOUTH);
L390: REPO | 
L391: BASE |         tryVisitNode(queue, originSection, outgoingWide, outgoingRegular, outgoingLocal, section.adjacentWest, GraphDirection.WEST);
L391: REPO |         visitNode(queue, section, GraphDirectionSet.of(direction), frame);
L392: BASE |         tryVisitNode(queue, originSection, outgoingWide, outgoingRegular, outgoingLocal, section.adjacentEast, GraphDirection.EAST);
L392: REPO |     }
L393: BASE |     }

L394: REPO |     private RenderSection getRenderSection(int x, int y, int z) {
L395: BASE |     private void tryVisitNode(WriteQueue<RenderSection> queue, RenderSection originSection, int outgoingWide, int outgoingRegular, int outgoingLocal, RenderSection section, int outgoingDirection) {
L395: REPO |         return this.sections.get(SectionPos.asLong(x, y, z));
L396: BASE |         // isn't usually null, but can be null if the bfs is happening during loading or unloading of chunks
L396: REPO |     }
L397: BASE |         if (section == null) {
L397: REPO | 
L398: BASE |             return;
L398: REPO | }
L399: BASE |         }
L399: REPO | <NO LINE>
L400: BASE | 
L400: REPO | <NO LINE>
L401: BASE |         var hasWidePath = GraphDirectionSet.contains(outgoingWide, outgoingDirection);
L401: REPO | <NO LINE>
L402: BASE |         var hasRegularPath = GraphDirectionSet.contains(outgoingRegular, outgoingDirection);
L402: REPO | <NO LINE>
L403: BASE |         var hasLocalPath = GraphDirectionSet.contains(outgoingLocal, outgoingDirection);
L403: REPO | <NO LINE>
L404: BASE | 
L404: REPO | <NO LINE>
L405: BASE |         // perform angular occlusion culling if enabled in general and locally
L405: REPO | <NO LINE>
L406: BASE |         // comment out to entirely disable angle-based occlusion culling, the other places are just supporting.
L406: REPO | <NO LINE>
L407: BASE |         if (originSection != null && hasRegularPath && !section.intersectSlopes(this.inBoundsOrigin, originSection, this.token)) {
L407: REPO | <NO LINE>
L408: BASE |             hasRegularPath = false;
L408: REPO | <NO LINE>
L409: BASE |             hasLocalPath = false;
L409: REPO | <NO LINE>
L410: BASE |         }
L410: REPO | <NO LINE>
L411: BASE | 
L411: REPO | <NO LINE>
L412: BASE |         if (!(hasWidePath || hasRegularPath || hasLocalPath)) {
L412: REPO | <NO LINE>
L413: BASE |             // no path to this neighbor from the current section, so skip it
L413: REPO | <NO LINE>
L414: BASE |             return;
L414: REPO | <NO LINE>
L415: BASE |         }
L415: REPO | <NO LINE>

L416: REPO | <NO LINE>
L417: BASE |         visitNode(queue, section, outgoingDirection, hasLocalPath, hasRegularPath, hasWidePath);

L418: BASE |     }

L419: BASE | 
L419: REPO | <NO LINE>
L420: BASE |     private void visitNode(WriteQueue<RenderSection> queue, RenderSection section, int outgoingDirection, boolean hasLocalPath, boolean hasRegularPath, boolean hasWidePath) {
L420: REPO | <NO LINE>
L421: BASE |         if (section.getSearchToken() != this.token) {
L421: REPO | <NO LINE>
L422: BASE |             // This is the first time we are visiting this section during the given token, so we must
L422: REPO | <NO LINE>
L423: BASE |             // reset the state.
L423: REPO | <NO LINE>
L424: BASE |             section.resetOnFirstVisit(this.token);
L424: REPO | <NO LINE>
L425: BASE | 
L425: REPO | <NO LINE>
L426: BASE |             // origin point of the chunk's bounding box (in view space)
L426: REPO | <NO LINE>
L427: BASE |             CameraTransform camera = this.viewport.getTransform();
L427: REPO | <NO LINE>
L428: BASE |             int ox = section.getOriginX() - camera.intX;
L428: REPO | <NO LINE>
L429: BASE |             int oy = section.getOriginY() - camera.intY;
L429: REPO | <NO LINE>
L430: BASE |             int oz = section.getOriginZ() - camera.intZ;
L430: REPO | <NO LINE>
L431: BASE | 
L431: REPO | <NO LINE>
L432: BASE |             // coordinates of the point to compare (in view space)
L432: REPO | <NO LINE>
L433: BASE |             // this is the closest point within the bounding box to the center (0, 0, 0)
L433: REPO | <NO LINE>
L434: BASE |             float dx = nearestToZero(ox - 1, ox + 17) - camera.fracX;
L434: REPO | <NO LINE>
L435: BASE |             float dy = nearestToZero(oy - 1, oy + 17) - camera.fracY;
L435: REPO | <NO LINE>
L436: BASE |             float dz = nearestToZero(oz - 1, oz + 17) - camera.fracZ;
L436: REPO | <NO LINE>
L437: BASE |             float xzThreshold = (dx * dx) + (dz * dz);
L437: REPO | <NO LINE>
L438: BASE |             float yThreshold = Math.abs(dy);
L438: REPO | <NO LINE>
L439: BASE | 
L439: REPO | <NO LINE>
L440: BASE |             // vanilla's "cylindrical fog" algorithm
L440: REPO | <NO LINE>
L441: BASE |             // max(length(distance.xz), abs(distance.y))
L441: REPO | <NO LINE>
L442: BASE |             if (testDistance(xzThreshold, yThreshold, this.searchDistanceRegular)) {
L442: REPO | <NO LINE>
L443: BASE |                 queue.enqueue(section);
L443: REPO | <NO LINE>
L444: BASE | 
L444: REPO | <NO LINE>
L445: BASE |                 if (!(testDistance(xzThreshold, yThreshold, this.searchDistanceLocal) && isWithinFrustum(this.viewport, section))) {
L445: REPO | <NO LINE>
L446: BASE |                     section.blockLocalIncoming();
L446: REPO | <NO LINE>
L447: BASE |                     hasLocalPath = false;
L447: REPO | <NO LINE>
L448: BASE |                 }
L448: REPO | <NO LINE>
L449: BASE |             }
L449: REPO | <NO LINE>
L450: BASE |         }
L450: REPO | <NO LINE>
L451: BASE | 
L451: REPO | <NO LINE>
L452: BASE |         var incomingSet = GraphDirectionSet.of(GraphDirection.opposite(outgoingDirection));
L452: REPO | <NO LINE>
L453: BASE |         if (hasWidePath) {
L453: REPO | <NO LINE>
L454: BASE |             section.addIncomingDirectionsWide(incomingSet);
L454: REPO | <NO LINE>
L455: BASE |             if (hasRegularPath) {
L455: REPO | <NO LINE>
L456: BASE |                 section.addIncomingDirectionsRegular(incomingSet);
L456: REPO | <NO LINE>
L457: BASE |                 if (hasLocalPath) {
L457: REPO | <NO LINE>
L458: BASE |                     section.addIncomingDirectionsLocal(incomingSet);
L458: REPO | <NO LINE>
L459: BASE |                 }
L459: REPO | <NO LINE>
L460: BASE |             }
L460: REPO | <NO LINE>
L461: BASE |         }
L461: REPO | <NO LINE>
L462: BASE |     }
L462: REPO | <NO LINE>
L463: BASE | 
L463: REPO | <NO LINE>
L464: BASE |     private static boolean testDistance(float xzThreshold, float yThreshold, float maxDistance) {
L464: REPO | <NO LINE>
L465: BASE |         return (xzThreshold < (maxDistance * maxDistance)) && (yThreshold < maxDistance);
L465: REPO | <NO LINE>
L466: BASE |     }
L466: REPO | <NO LINE>
L467: BASE | 
L467: REPO | <NO LINE>
L468: BASE |     @SuppressWarnings("ManualMinMaxCalculation") // we know what we are doing.
L468: REPO | <NO LINE>
L469: BASE |     private static int nearestToZero(int min, int max) {
L469: REPO | <NO LINE>
L470: BASE |         // this compiles to slightly better code than Math.min(Math.max(0, min), max)
L470: REPO | <NO LINE>
L471: BASE |         int clamped = 0;
L471: REPO | <NO LINE>
L472: BASE |         if (min > 0) {
L472: REPO | <NO LINE>
L473: BASE |             clamped = min;
L473: REPO | <NO LINE>
L474: BASE |         }
L474: REPO | <NO LINE>
L475: BASE |         if (max < 0) {
L475: REPO | <NO LINE>
L476: BASE |             clamped = max;
L476: REPO | <NO LINE>
L477: BASE |         }
L477: REPO | <NO LINE>
L478: BASE |         return clamped;
L478: REPO | <NO LINE>
L479: BASE |     }

L481: REPO | <NO LINE>
L482: BASE |         return viewport.isBoxVisibleLooser(section.getCenterX(), section.getCenterY(), section.getCenterZ());
L482: REPO | <NO LINE>
L483: BASE |     }

L484: REPO | <NO LINE>
L485: BASE |     private void visitAll(RenderSection section) {
L485: REPO | <NO LINE>
L486: BASE |         this.visitorWide.visit(section, true);
L486: REPO | <NO LINE>
L487: BASE |         this.visitorRegular.visit(section, true);
L487: REPO | <NO LINE>
L488: BASE |         this.visitorLocal.visit(section, true);
L488: REPO | <NO LINE>
L489: BASE |     }
L489: REPO | <NO LINE>
L490: BASE | 
L490: REPO | <NO LINE>
L491: BASE |     // This method visits sections near the origin that are not in the path of the graph traversal
L491: REPO | <NO LINE>
L492: BASE |     // but have bounding boxes that may intersect with the frustum. It does this additional check
L492: REPO | <NO LINE>
L493: BASE |     // for all neighboring, even diagonally neighboring, sections around the origin to render them
L493: REPO | <NO LINE>
L494: BASE |     // if their extended bounding box is visible, and they may render large models that extend
L494: REPO | <NO LINE>
L495: BASE |     // outside the 16x16x16 base volume of the section.
L495: REPO | <NO LINE>
L496: BASE |     private void addNearbySections(Viewport viewport) {
L496: REPO | <NO LINE>
L497: BASE |         var origin = viewport.getChunkCoord();
L497: REPO | <NO LINE>
L498: BASE |         var originX = origin.getX();
L498: REPO | <NO LINE>
L499: BASE |         var originY = origin.getY();
L499: REPO | <NO LINE>
L500: BASE |         var originZ = origin.getZ();
L500: REPO | <NO LINE>
L501: BASE | 
L501: REPO | <NO LINE>
L502: BASE |         for (var dx = -1; dx <= 1; dx++) {
L502: REPO | <NO LINE>
L503: BASE |             for (var dy = -1; dy <= 1; dy++) {
L503: REPO | <NO LINE>
L504: BASE |                 for (var dz = -1; dz <= 1; dz++) {
L504: REPO | <NO LINE>
L505: BASE |                     if (dx == 0 && dy == 0 && dz == 0) {
L505: REPO | <NO LINE>
L506: BASE |                         continue;
L506: REPO | <NO LINE>
L507: BASE |                     }
L507: REPO | <NO LINE>
L508: BASE | 
L508: REPO | <NO LINE>
L509: BASE |                     var section = this.getRenderSection(originX + dx, originY + dy, originZ + dz);
L509: REPO | <NO LINE>
L510: BASE | 
L510: REPO | <NO LINE>
L511: BASE |                     // additionally render not yet visited but visible sections
L511: REPO | <NO LINE>
L512: BASE |                     if (section != null && section.getSearchToken() != this.token && isWithinNearbySectionFrustum(viewport, section)) {
L512: REPO | <NO LINE>
L513: BASE |                         // reset state on first visit, but don't enqueue
L513: REPO | <NO LINE>
L514: BASE |                         section.resetOnFirstVisit(this.token);
L514: REPO | <NO LINE>
L515: BASE | 
L515: REPO | <NO LINE>
L516: BASE |                         this.visitAll(section);
L516: REPO | <NO LINE>
L517: BASE |                     }
L517: REPO | <NO LINE>
L518: BASE |                 }
L518: REPO | <NO LINE>
L519: BASE |             }
L519: REPO | <NO LINE>
L520: BASE |         }
L520: REPO | <NO LINE>
L521: BASE |     }

L522: BASE | 
L522: REPO | <NO LINE>
L523: BASE |     private void init(WriteQueue<RenderSection> queue) {
L523: REPO | <NO LINE>
L524: BASE |         if (this.origin.getY() < this.level.getMinSectionY()) {
L524: REPO | <NO LINE>
L525: BASE |             // below the level
L525: REPO | <NO LINE>
L526: BASE |             this.outOfWorldRadius = 0;
L526: REPO | <NO LINE>

L409: REPO |         this.fadeTime = relativeBuiltTime;
L410: BASE |         long borrows = parallel_unsigned_lt_msbs((pathAngles & ANGLES_MAX_MASK) >> ANGLE_BITS, pathAngles & ANGLES_MIN_MASK);
L410: REPO |     }
L411: BASE |         if (borrows != 0) {

L417: REPO | <NO LINE>

L424: BASE | 
L424: REPO | <NO LINE>
L425: BASE |     /**
L425: REPO | <NO LINE>
L426: BASE |      * Performs a parallel unsigned less-than comparison (a < b) for 6 10-bit lanes.
L426: REPO | <NO LINE>
L427: BASE |      *
L427: REPO | <NO LINE>
L428: BASE |      * @param a 6 packed 10-bit values
L428: REPO | <NO LINE>
L429: BASE |      * @param b 6 packed 10-bit values
L429: REPO | <NO LINE>
L430: BASE |      * @return A long with the MSB of each lane (bit 9, 19, 29, ...) set if a_k < b_k.
L430: REPO | <NO LINE>
L431: BASE |      * <p>
L431: REPO | <NO LINE>
L432: BASE |      * Based on `vhaddu8(~a, b)` (LTU_VARIANT 0) from:
L432: REPO | <NO LINE>
L433: BASE |      * <a href="https://stackoverflow.com/a/68717720/3694">Stackoverflow</a>
L433: REPO | <NO LINE>
L434: BASE |      * Citing Peter L. Montgomery's observation
L434: REPO | <NO LINE>
L435: BASE |      * <a href="https://groups.google.com/d/msg/comp.arch/gXFuGZtZKag/_5yrz2zDbe4J">comp.arch, 2000/02/11</a>:
L435: REPO | <NO LINE>
L436: BASE |      * (A+B)/2 = (A AND B) + (A XOR B)/2.
L436: REPO | <NO LINE>
L437: BASE |      * The MSB of (A+B)/2 is the same as the carry-out of (A+B),
L437: REPO | <NO LINE>
L438: BASE |      * and `vhaddu(~a, b)` calculates `(~a+b)/2`, which sets the MSB if `b > a`.
L438: REPO | <NO LINE>
L439: BASE |      */
L439: REPO | <NO LINE>
L440: BASE |     private static long parallel_unsigned_lt_msbs(long a, long b) {
L440: REPO | <NO LINE>
L441: BASE |         // MSB (sign bit) for each 10-bit lane
L441: REPO | <NO LINE>
L442: BASE |         final long LANE_MSB = 1L << (ANGLE_BITS - 1);
L442: REPO | <NO LINE>
L443: BASE |         final long LANE_MSB_MASK = (LANE_MSB << (ANGLE_BITS * 0)) |
L443: REPO | <NO LINE>
L444: BASE |                 (LANE_MSB << (ANGLE_BITS * 1)) |
L444: REPO | <NO LINE>
L445: BASE |                 (LANE_MSB << (ANGLE_BITS * 2)) |
L445: REPO | <NO LINE>
L446: BASE |                 (LANE_MSB << (ANGLE_BITS * 3)) |
L446: REPO | <NO LINE>
L447: BASE |                 (LANE_MSB << (ANGLE_BITS * 4)) |
L447: REPO | <NO LINE>
L448: BASE |                 (LANE_MSB << (ANGLE_BITS * 5));
L448: REPO | <NO LINE>
L449: BASE |         // All bits *except* the MSB for each 10-bit lane
L449: REPO | <NO LINE>

L3: BASE | When submitting a pull request, you are granting [JellySquid](https://jellysquid.me) the right to license

L5: BASE | 
L5: REPO | ## Attribution
L6: BASE | Without exception, it is required that all code being submitted to the project is of your original work. We
L6: REPO |

L7: REPO | - Mercurizer authors: [Edonme](https://edonme.dev/)
L8: BASE | circumstances.

L10: BASE | If you have any questions about these terms, please [get in contact with us](https://caffeinemc.net/discord).
L10: REPO | ## Code Style
L12: BASE | ### Code Style
L12: REPO | - Use 4 spaces for indentation.
L13: BASE | 
L13: REPO | - Keep changes scoped and consistent with the existing module layout.
L14: BASE | When contributing source code to the project, ensure that you make consistent use of our code style guidelines. These
L14: REPO | - Prefer edits in `common/`, `fabric/`, and `frapi/` over introducing parallel source trees.
L15: BASE | guidelines are based on the [Google code style guidelines for Java](https://google.github.io/styleguide/javaguide.html),
L15: REPO | 
L16: BASE | with some minor changes, as described below.
L16: REPO | ## Pull Requests
L18: BASE | - Use 4 spaces for indentation, not tabs. Avoid lines which exceed 120 characters.
L18: REPO | - Describe the behavioral change clearly.
L19: BASE | - Avoid deeply nested conditional logic, and prefer breaking out to separate functions when possible.
L19: REPO | - Link any related issue.
L20: BASE |     - If you are using more than three levels of indentation, you should likely consider restructuring your code.
L20: REPO | - Keep documentation and metadata in sync when changing user-facing behavior.
L21: BASE |     - Branches which are only exceptionally or very rarely taken should remain concise. When this is not possible,
L21: REPO | <NO LINE>
L22: BASE |       prefer breaking out to a new method (where it makes sense) as this helps the compiler better optimize the code.
L22: REPO | <NO LINE>
L23: BASE | - Use `this` to qualify method and field access, as it avoids some ambiguity in certain contexts.
L23: REPO | <NO LINE>
L24: BASE | 
L24: REPO | <NO LINE>
L25: BASE | We also provide these code styles as [EditorConfig](https://editorconfig.org/) files, which most Java IDEs will
L25: REPO | <NO LINE>
L26: BASE | automatically detect and make use of.
L26: REPO | <NO LINE>
L27: BASE | 
L27: REPO | <NO LINE>
L28: BASE | #### Unsafe code
L28: REPO | <NO LINE>
L29: BASE | 
L29: REPO | <NO LINE>
L30: BASE | There are some additional guidelines for using unsafe code in Java, which are aimed at improving code readability and
L30: REPO | <NO LINE>
L31: BASE | debugging. Most developers will not need to read this section unless they are working with bindings to native libraries.
L31: REPO | <NO LINE>
L32: BASE | 
L32: REPO | <NO LINE>
L33: BASE | - The use of unsafe operations in general should be avoided unless strictly necessary, or where there is otherwise a
L33: REPO | <NO LINE>
L34: BASE |   clear performance advantage.
L34: REPO | <NO LINE>
L35: BASE |     - Unsafe code is often _slower_ than safe code, since the Hotspot compiler only contains basic intrinsics and is
L35: REPO | <NO LINE>
L36: BASE |       very pessimistic about optimizing such code.
L36: REPO | <NO LINE>
L37: BASE |     - Array bounds checks are almost never the bottleneck of your code. But memory layout often is, and unsafe code
L37: REPO | <NO LINE>
L38: BASE |       gives you explicit control over it.
L38: REPO | <NO LINE>
L39: BASE | - The invariants of public methods which expose unsafe operations MUST be documented.
L39: REPO | <NO LINE>
L40: BASE |     - An example of a safety invariant would be if passing a null pointer value or invalid array index to a method which
L40: REPO | <NO LINE>
L41: BASE |       uses unsafe operations would cause an invalid memory access.
L41: REPO | <NO LINE>
L42: BASE |     - The caller of your method should know exactly what is needed to avoid undefined or invalid behavior. 
L42: REPO | <NO LINE>
L43: BASE | - The invariants of unsafe methods SHOULD be checked where possible. The use of runtime checking SHOULD be configurable
L43: REPO | <NO LINE>
L44: BASE |   at application launch.
L44: REPO | <NO LINE>
L45: BASE |     - The typical way to implement these checks is to keep a `private static final boolean CHECKS = /* ... */;` field in
L45: REPO | <NO LINE>
L46: BASE |       the class which controls the use of runtime checking.
L46: REPO | <NO LINE>
L47: BASE |     - These checks can then be wrapped within an if-statement which allows them to be skipped if runtime error checking
L47: REPO | <NO LINE>
L48: BASE |       is disabled. This also allows the compiler to easily optimize them away.
L48: REPO | <NO LINE>
L49: BASE | - When plain integer types are used to store pointers to memory addresses, their variables MUST be prefixed, and a 
L49: REPO | <NO LINE>
L50: BASE |   comment MUST be added to indicate their underlying type.
L50: REPO | <NO LINE>
L51: BASE |     - For example, the declaration `int* value;` in C code would be represented as `long pValue; // int*` in Java code.
L51: REPO | <NO LINE>
L52: BASE |     - For chains of pointers, multiple prefixes should be used. For example, `char** strings;` in C code would become
L52: REPO | <NO LINE>
L53: BASE |       `long ppStrings; // char**` in Java code.
L53: REPO | <NO LINE>
L54: BASE | 
L54: REPO | <NO LINE>
L55: BASE | ### Pull Requests
L55: REPO | <NO LINE>
L56: BASE | 
L56: REPO | <NO LINE>
L57: BASE | Your pull request should include a brief description of the changes it makes and link to any open issues which it
L57: REPO | <NO LINE>
L58: BASE | resolves. You should also ensure that your code is well documented where it is non-trivial, and that it follows our code
L58: REPO | <NO LINE>
L59: BASE | style guidelines.
L59: REPO | <NO LINE>
L60: BASE |

L61: REPO | <NO LINE>

L62: REPO | <NO LINE>

L61: BASE | ## ✅ Hardware Compatibility
L61: REPO | <NO LINE>
L62: BASE | 
L62: REPO | <NO LINE>
L63: BASE | We only provide official support for graphics cards which have up-to-date drivers that are compatible with OpenGL 4.5
L63: REPO | <NO LINE>
L64: BASE | or newer. Most graphics cards released in the past 12 years will meet these requirements, including the following:
L64: REPO | <NO LINE>
L65: BASE | 
L65: REPO | <NO LINE>
L66: BASE | - AMD Radeon HD 7000 Series (GCN 1) or newer
L66: REPO | <NO LINE>
L67: BASE | - NVIDIA GeForce 400 Series (Fermi) or newer
L67: REPO | <NO LINE>
L68: BASE | - Intel HD Graphics 500 Series (Skylake) or newer
L68: REPO | <NO LINE>
L69: BASE | 
L69: REPO | <NO LINE>
L70: BASE | Nearly all graphics cards that are already compatible with Minecraft (which requires OpenGL 3.3) should also work
L70: REPO | <NO LINE>
L71: BASE | with Sodium. But our team cannot ensure compatibility or provide support for older graphics cards, and they may
L71: REPO | <NO LINE>
L72: BASE | not work with future versions of Sodium.
L72: REPO | <NO LINE>
L73: BASE | 
L73: REPO | <NO LINE>
L74: BASE | #### OpenGL Compatibility Layers
L74: REPO | <NO LINE>
L75: BASE | 
L75: REPO | <NO LINE>
L76: BASE | Devices which need to use OpenGL translation layers (such as GL4ES, ANGLE, etc.) are not supported and will very likely
L76: REPO | <NO LINE>
L77: BASE | not work with Sodium. These translation layers do not implement required functionality, and they suffer from underlying
L77: REPO | <NO LINE>
L78: BASE | driver bugs which cannot be worked around.
L78: REPO | <NO LINE>
